Some cantons that have been passed on to science in power, have several universities, have zero separations for BiH, and are only turned into concerts for diplomas without any basis, without any validity that these diplomas carry. There are five out of ten cantons that have zero separations for scientific research and at all those who single out, I said that's 0.06%. That's very small. Where are we and what's our perspective if we continue like this? Are we even able to participate in projects, scientific projects organised by the EU? In Framework Project 7 for 2014 BiH is required an entry card of 4.6m euros, with a total of 3.5m euros for FP 7 for six years. Therefore, reforms are urgently needed in BiH when it comes to higher education, i.e. scientific and research work. The request of the BiH Council of Ministers for consideration of the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Excise Taxation Act in Bosnia and Herzegovina under the haste procedure, in accordance with Article 127. ParlaMint-BA_2014-04-15-0.u20852	Mr. Chairman, esteemed colleagues and colleagues, MPs, distinguished guests, I would like to welcome you. I am glad that today we have on the agenda a draft law on amendments and additions to the Excise Act in BiH. Thank you for putting that on the agenda today. First I'm going to say something about needs, about the essence of these changes. In fact, the process of harmonising the taxation of tobacco processing in BiH with EU standards began as early as 01/07.2009, when a special excise duty of 0.15 KM per pack of 20 pieces was applied in addition to the proportional excise duty rate on cigarettes of 42% at a retail price, with that amount increasing by an additional 0.15 KM per pack of cigarettes from 01.01 each year. Since 01.01.2014 the special excise duty is 0,90 KM per pack of 20 pieces, or 45 marks for 1,000 pieces of cigarettes. The increase in the tax burden of cigarettes in the first year of application of the new law follows an adequate growth in revenue on this basis, so for example, in 2009, by half of the year the increase was 85 million, in 2010 167 million, in 2011, that growth amounted to 100 million, in 2012, 42 million, in order to record a fall in revenue on this basis of 31 million KM. From the above, it is evident that the continued increase in excise burden did not bring an adequate growth in income, and this made us, first on the Management Board of the Direct Taxation Board, and also through the Council of Ministers, to see how we can improve, or come up with, excises harmonised with standards, with EU directives, and also to ensure income growth and reduce tax evasion on that basis. By observing data on excise stamps issued on tobacco processing, it is evident that the number of excise stamps issued on tobacco processing has been constantly dropping since the start of application of the new Act. So in 2009, 534 million pieces of excise stamps were issued on tobacco processing, in 2010 512 million pieces, in 2011 473 million, in 2012, in 415, 2013 328 million pieces. The drop in the number of excise stamps issued for tobacco processing and the drop in revenue on this basis is a signal that cigarette consumption in BiH, on the one hand, is on the one hand, as well as the growth of the irregular tobacco processing market in BiH. According to data from the Directorate for indirect taxation since the entry into force of this Excise Act, there has been a single increase in imports of so-called ‘indicative taxation’. Rizzles, i.e. cigarettes of cigarette-swallowing paper. So 16.9 tonnes were imported in 2010, 90.3 tonnes, 301.5 tonnes in 2012, and 482 tonnes in 2013. Which means that there's an illegal market booming, and that's why we have too much evac, tax evasion, out of a couple of tens of millions of KMs. And that was the reason for us to change this law. Why are we looking for an emergency procedure? It's only about amending five members of the Law. On the one hand, we had, therefore, a consensus on the Governing Board of the Directorate for Direct Taxation, full consensus in the Council of Ministers, it is a matter of harmonising with EU directives, we have a positive opinion of the Directorate for European Integration. And to be perfectly honest, it is also a matter of respect, so letter norms intending with the International Monetary Fund, which also requires that order be introduced into this area. For these reasons, I ask you to accept the emergency procedure, because this is a struggle for income and for preventing, or for reducing tax evasion. It's no secret that little house-making workshops have already been created. It is no secret that we also have poor co-operation of inspections of different levels, let's say market inspections at the level of entities with tax administrations and mutual tax administrations, but we think this law will provide a few dozen million marks in the budget of the BiH government. And that's why I'm asking you to accept emergency procedures. The important thing is, we've been looking for an emergency procedure for the reason... of the IMF's 29th session, probably going to be transferred in the first week of May. You know that from the arrangement with the IMF of the BiH government institution at the state level they don't take any KM to the Budget Authority, but given the situation in the entity budgets and obligations that I am asking you to support the emergency procedure, because we will do a good job for everyone. So now that we're talking about FIPA, are we going to measure the efficiency of FIPA by how much it's held the promotion, the event, how many potential investors have contacted, how many brochures, flyers, etc., or are we going to measure it by how much investment has entered the country? I believe you'll agree that the dilemma is serious. Namely, why is he serious? FIPA is an institution established with the role of promoting investments. So someone should first develop the first BiH development strategy and then define in this context the place of foreign direct investment in encouraging, that is, the state of development. Because, believe me, none of the countries that had access to foreign direct investment were unthinkable, beyond the context of their development, successful and vice versa, those who had their own concept of development and foreign direct investment had success. Let's not say that the Czech Republic has set up its concept of development based on foreign direct investment, especially the automotive industry, and has made the automotive industry one of the most powerful in all of Europe. But that's why almost all of the world's automotive companies are present in the Czech Republic. And basically this shows us that you can't manage foreign direct investment, so you can't promote it, regardless of the overall context of development. 75 So we don't have development strategies. Mrs. Trninić will now tell me, and why can't we base this strategy on entity plans? Well, you can do that, but that's immediately a fallout effect, right, that's not necessarily optimal effects. And you practically have a FIPA position that she needs to promote something that doesn't exist. It was easy to operate FIPA at a time when privatisation was conducted. At that time, foreign direct investment entered through privatisation channels. We had a so-called. The smoke model, we were pushing investment projects, so factories, ArcelorMittal, Natron- Hayat, we were offering Celeks, we were offering the Voter etc., we were pushing towards investors, and that was quite a bit of a simple activity. You had a tangible investment, you had a tangible investment project, you offered it to foreign investors. Now we have to, then 75% of the investment went through privatisation, now we have to go to greenfield investments, we have to go to new investments. And above all, we need to raise the country's attractiveness to that level so investors are attracted and that they define projects that they will implement in BiH. And for that step, we don't even have a strategy, we don't have an institution, we don't have a level, so the entire value chain is an adequate structure. The foreign trade and economic relations ministry also does not have a strategy that would be a strategy for foreign direct investment, which would be the basis for FIPA to act. Of course, all other institutions that contribute to this in a certain way, such as economic diplomacy, such as tax institutions, etc., etc. So, to just go back to this key question, whether FIPA is successfully communicating, whether it's sending reports of what it did, etc., that's less important, it's a key problem for us to make a good context for realising foreign direct investment, because someone said we don't have an investment. We in the last 4.5 years have practically only the activity of existing investors that reinvest something, so we record it, or they take loans from abroad, banks, that, intra-corporate loans, so we record it as investments. The latest data from the Central Bank that last year was 470 million of these investments. It's at your level of statistical error, is it, that's 3% of your gross domestic product, no, actually 2, below 2%, is it? Then, the total investment level of 10.5 billion, 7.5 billion dollars practically stagnated over the last few years. And we really have a big problem in that area. So, I appreciate the high findings of the Audit Office, because it revealed to us the problem that exists in that area, but I don't think we can blame FIPA for the low efficiency. And secondly, that actually FIPA doesn't make any sense unless the context for managing foreign direct investment is changed, because then it's just a bureaucratic, technocrat organization that sends papers, receives people, can't solve problems, or has something new to say to people. So, I think the basic value of this impact study is that it exposes this problem, and I think it should be much more place in the recommendations to find exactly what you've come up with in revealing the empty space that it represents, which should be a strategic basis for FIPA's action. Thank you very much.
